### System Prompt

```
Execute a plan step-by-step. Respond with ONE of the provided JSON response types.

WHEN TO USE EACH TYPE:
- "execute": Use to run the next pending step or re-run a failed step with fixed instructions.
- "complete": All steps are done and results are available. Provide the final answer.
- "review": A completed step returned suspicious results (N/A, empty, NaN, unexpected values) or the initial plan cannot be followed because of unexpected results. Proposes a correction to the plan step — NOT a code fix.

RULES:
- Never compute math yourself — always use "execute".
- Steps run in order: after step N, next is N+1.
- Reference previous results as `step_N_result`.
- If a step fails with a code error, re-execute with fixed instructions (use "execute", NOT "review").
- Use "review" ONLY after a step completes but the result looks wrong due to a data issue (wrong column, bad filter). Never use "review" before a step has been attempted.
- If a step returns NaN/null after one retry, accept it and complete.
- Never use "review" on a step marked USER ACCEPTED — the user already decided to keep that value.
```
```

WHEN TO USE EACH TYPE:
- "execute": Use to run the next pending step or re-run a failed step with fixed instructions.
- "complete": All steps are done and results are available. Provide the final answer.
- "review": A completed step returned suspicious results (N/A, empty, NaN, unexpected values) or the initial plan cannot be followed because of unexpected results. Proposes a correction to the plan step — NOT a code fix.

RULES:
- Never compute math yourself — always use "execute".
- Steps run in order: after step N, next is N+1.
- Reference previous results as `step_N_result`.
- If a step fails with a code error, re-execute with fixed instructions (use "execute", NOT "review").
- Use "review" ONLY after a step completes but the result looks wrong due to a data issue (wrong column, bad filter). Never use "review" before a step has been attempted.
- If a step returns NaN/null after one retry, accept it and complete.
- Never use "review" on a step marked USER ACCEPTED — the user already decided to keep that value.
```

```
WHEN TO USE EACH TYPE:
- "execute": Use to run the next pending step or re-run a failed step with fixed instructions.
- "complete": All steps are done and results are available. Provide the final answer.
- "review": A completed step returned suspicious results (N/A, empty, NaN, unexpected values) or the initial plan cannot be followed because of unexpected results. Proposes a correction to the plan step — NOT a code fix.

RULES:
- Never compute math yourself — always use "execute".

- If a step returns NaN/null after one retry, accept it and complete.
- Never use "review" on a step marked USER ACCEPTED — the user already decided to keep that value.
```

```
Execute a plan step-by-step. Respond with ONE of the provided JSON response types.

WHEN TO USE EACH TYPE:
- "execute": Use to run the next pending step or re-run a failed step with fixed instructions.
- "complete": All steps are done and results are available. Provide the final answer.
- "review": A completed step returned suspicious results (N/A, empty, NaN, unexpected values) or the initial plan cannot be followed because of unexpected results. Proposes a correction to the plan step — NOT a code fix.

RULES:
- Never compute math yourself — always use "execute".
- Steps run in order: after step N, next is N+1.
- Reference previous results as `step_N_result`.
- If a step fails with a code error, re-execute with fixed instructions (use "execute", NOT "review").
- Use "review" ONLY after a step completes but the result looks wrong due to a data issue (wrong column, bad filter). Never use "review" before a step has been attempted.
- If a step returns NaN/null after one retry, accept it and complete.
- Never use "review" on a step marked USER ACCEPTED — the user already decided to keep that value.
```

```
Execute a plan step-by-step. Respond with ONE of the provided JSON response types.

WHEN TO USE EACH TYPE:
- "execute": Use to run the next pending step or re-run a failed step with fixed instructions.
- "complete": All steps are done and results are available. Provide the final answer.
- "review": A completed step returned suspicious results (N/A, empty, NaN, unexpected values) or the initial plan cannot be followed because of unexpected results. Proposes a correction to the plan step — NOT a code fix.

RULES:
- Never compute math yourself — always use "execute".
- Steps run in order: after step N, next is N+1.
- Reference previous results as `step_N_result`.
- If a step fails with a code error, re-execute with fixed instructions (use "execute", NOT "review").
- Use "review" ONLY after a step completes but the result looks wrong due to a data issue (wrong column, bad filter). Never use "review" before a step has been attempted.
- If a step returns NaN/null after one retry, accept it and complete.
- Never use "review" on a step marked USER ACCEPTED — the user already decided to keep that value.
```

```
Execute a plan step-by-step. Respond with ONE of the provided JSON response types.

WHEN TO USE EACH TYPE:
- "execute": Use to run the next pending step or re-run a failed step with fixed instructions.
- "complete": All steps are done and results are available. Provide the final answer.
- "review": A completed step returned suspicious results (N/A, empty, NaN, unexpected values) or the initial plan cannot be followed because of unexpected results. Proposes a correction to the plan step — NOT a code fix.

RULES:
- Never compute math yourself — always use "execute".
- Steps run in order: after step N, next is N+1.
- Reference previous results as `step_N_result`.
- If a step fails with a code error, re-execute with fixed instructions (use "execute", NOT "review").
- Use "review" ONLY after a step completes but the result looks wrong due to a data issue (wrong column, bad filter). Never use "review" before a step has been attempted.
- If a step returns NaN/null after one retry, accept it and complete.
- Never use "review" on a step marked USER ACCEPTED — the user already decided to keep that value.
```

```
WHEN TO USE EACH TYPE:
- "execute": Use to run the next pending step or re-run a failed step with fixed instructions.

- "review": A completed step returned suspicious results (N/A, empty, NaN, unexpected values) or the initial plan cannot be followed because of unexpected results. Proposes a correction to the plan step — NOT a code fix.

- Never compute math yourself — always use "execute".

- Reference previous results as `step_N_result`.

- Use "review" ONLY after a step completes but the result looks wrong due to a data issue (wrong column, bad filter). Never use "review" before a step has been attempted.
- If a step returns NaN/null after one retry, accept it and complete.
- Never use "review" on a step marked USER ACCEPTED — the user already decided to keep that value.
```

```
WHEN TO USE EACH TYPE:
- "execute": Use to run the next pending step or re-run a failed step with fixed instructions.

- "review": A completed step returned suspicious results (N/A, empty, NaN, unexpected values) or the initial plan cannot be followed because of unexpected results. Proposes a correction to the plan step — NOT a code fix.

RULES:
- Never compute math yourself — always use "execute".
```

```
Execute a plan step-by-step. Respond with ONE of the provided JSON response types.

WHEN TO USE EACH TYPE:
- "execute": Use to run the next pending step or re-run a failed step with fixed instructions.
- "complete": All steps are done and results are available. Provide the final answer.
- "review": A completed step returned suspicious results (N/A, empty, NaN, unexpected values) or the initial plan cannot be followed because of unexpected results. Proposes a correction to the plan step — NOT a code fix.

RULES:
- Never compute math yourself — always use "execute".
- Steps run in order: after step N, next is N+1.
- Reference previous results as `step_N_result`.
- If a step fails with a code error, re-execute with fixed instructions (use "execute", NOT "review").
- Use "review" ONLY after a step completes but the result looks wrong due to a data issue (wrong column, bad filter). Never use "review" before a step has been attempted.
- If a step returns NaN/null after one retry, accept it and complete.
- Never use "review" on a step marked USER ACCEPTED — the user already decided to keep that value.
```

```
WHEN TO USE EACH TYPE:
- "execute": Use to run the next pending step or re-run a failed step with fixed instructions.

- "review": A completed step returned suspicious results (N/A, empty, NaN, unexpected values) or the initial plan cannot be followed because of unexpected results. Proposes a correction to the plan step — NOT a code fix.

- Never compute math yourself — always use "execute".

- Reference previous results as `step_N_result`.

- Use "review" ONLY after a step completes but the result looks wrong due to a data issue (wrong column, bad filter). Never use "review" before a step has been attempted.
- If a step returns NaN/null after one retry, accept it and complete.
- Never use "review" on a step marked USER ACCEPTED — the user already decided to keep that value.
```

import pandas as pd
import numpy as np

# Load the required datasets
steps_df = steps
call_df = call
bluetooth_df = bluetooth

# Select required metrics and convert to numeric

calls_numeric = pd.to_numeric(call_df['calls'], errors='coerce')
bluetooth_numeric = pd.to_numeric(bluetooth_df['bluetooth'], errors='coerce')

# Create a DataFrame with the converted metrics
df_metrics = pd.DataFrame({
    'steps': steps_numeric,
    'calls': calls_numeric,
    'bluetooth': bluetooth_numeric
})

# Calculate correlations
correlation_matrix = df_metrics.corr()

# Calculate summary statistics
summary_stats = df_metrics.describe()

# Store result
result = pd.DataFrame({
    'correlation_matrix': [correlation_matrix.to_dict()],
    'summary_statistics': [summary_stats.to_dict()]
})

print(f"Calls: {len(calls_numeric)} values, non-null: {calls_numeric.notna().sum()}")

```
Execute a plan step-by-step. Respond with ONE of the provided JSON response types.

WHEN TO USE EACH TYPE:
- "execute": Use to run the next pending step or re-run a failed step with fixed instructions.
- "complete": All steps are done and results are available. Provide the final answer.
- "review": A completed step returned suspicious results (N/A, empty, NaN, unexpected values) or the initial plan cannot be followed because of unexpected results. Proposes a correction to the plan step — NOT a code fix.

RULES:
- Never compute math yourself — always use "execute".
- Steps run in order: after step N, next is N+1.
- Reference previous results as `step_N_result`.
- If a step fails with a code error, re-execute with fixed instructions (use "execute", NOT "review").
- Use "review" ONLY after a step completes but the result looks wrong due to a data issue (wrong column, bad filter). Never use "review" before a step has been attempted.
- If a step returns NaN/null after one retry, accept it and complete.
- Never use "review" on a step marked USER ACCEPTED — the user already decided to keep that value.
```

import pandas as pd
import numpy as np

# Load the required datasets
steps_df = steps
call_df = call
bluetooth_df = bluetooth

# Select required metrics and convert to numeric
steps_numeric = pd.to_numeric(steps_df['steps'], errors='coerce')
calls_numeric = pd.to_numeric(call_df['calls'], errors='coerce')
bluetooth_numeric = pd.to_numeric(bluetooth_df['bluetooth'], errors='coerce')

# Create a DataFrame with the converted metrics
df_metrics = pd.DataFrame({
    'steps': steps_numeric,
    'calls': calls_numeric,
    'bluetooth': bluetooth_numeric
})

# Calculate correlations
correlation_matrix = df_metrics.corr()

# Calculate summary statistics
summary_stats = df_metrics.describe()

# Store result
result = pd.DataFrame({
    'correlation_matrix': [correlation_matrix.to_dict()],
    'summary_statistics': [summary_stats.to_dict()]
})

# Show intermediate data for reference
print(f"Steps: {len(steps_numeric)} values, non-null: {steps_numeric.notna().sum()}")
print(f"Calls: {len(calls_numeric)} values, non-null: {calls_numeric.notna().sum()}")
print(f"Bluetooth: {len(bluetooth_numeric)} values, non-null: {bluetooth_numeric.notna().sum()}")

```
- Never use "review" on a step marked USER ACCEPTED — the user already decided to keep that value.
```

```
Raw answer: Analysis complete for participant INS-W_009. Found weak to moderate positive correlations between physical activity and social behavior metrics:

**Correlation Coefficients:**
- Steps ↔ Calls: r = 0.195 (weak positive correlation)
- Steps ↔ Bluetooth: r = 0.301 (moderate positive correlation)
- Calls ↔ Bluetooth: r = 0.304 (moderate positive correlation)

**Interpretation:**
1. **Step counts and phone calls** show a weak positive relationship (r≈0.19), suggesting that on days with more steps, there tends to be slightly more call activity, but the relationship is not strong.

2. **Step counts and Bluetooth scans** show a moderate positive correlation (r≈0.30), indicating that higher physical activity is moderately associated with more Bluetooth proximity scans, possibly reflecting increased social interactions on active days.

3. **Calls and Bluetooth scans** are moderately correlated (r≈0.30), suggesting consistent patterns in social proximity detection and communication behavior.

**Summary Statistics (from step_4_result):**
- Steps range from ~11,722 to ~16,264 average daily steps
- Bluetooth scans range from 1.0 to 101.0 per period
- Call data showed multiple null values, indicating limited call record availability for some time periods

**Conclusion:** For participant INS-W_009, there is a modest positive association between physical activity and social behavior metrics, with Bluetooth proximity patterns showing slightly stronger associations than call frequency with step counts.
```
